# Research: subset-b-000592

Grouped research for ceph-client Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering MMC/SD host controllers, MTD flash/NAND/partition bindings, mux bindings, Ethernet/MDIO/PHY bindings, and Bluetooth controller bindings. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mmc/snps,dwcmshc-sdhci.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Synopsys Designware Mobile Storage Host Controller`. In this tree it is a MMC/SD/SDHCI storage host controller binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mmc/snps,dwcmshc-sdhci.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `rockchip,rk3528-dwcmshc`, `rockchip,rk3562-dwcmshc`, `rockchip,rk3576-dwcmshc`, `rockchip,rk3588-dwcmshc`, `sophgo,sg2044-dwcmshc`, `sophgo,sg2042-dwcmshc`, `canaan,k230-emmc`, `canaan,k230-sdio`, `hpe,gsc-dwcmshc`, `rockchip,rk3568-dwcmshc`, `snps,dwcmshc-sdhci`, `sophgo,cv1800b-dwcmshc`, `sophgo,sg2002-dwcmshc`, `thead,th1520-dwcmshc`, `eswin,eic7700-dwcmshc`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `power-domains`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `canaan,usb-phy`, `rockchip,txclk-tapnum`, `eswin,hsp-sp-csr`, `eswin,drive-impedance-ohms`, `hpe,gxp-sysreg`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `interrupts`: interrupt line routing; `clocks`: clock provider phandles; `clock-names`: clock input names; `resets`: reset controller phandles; `reset-names`: reset line names; `power-domains`: generic power-domain attachment; `canaan,usb-phy`: Phandle to the Canaan K230 USB PHY node required for k230-emmc/sdio.; `rockchip,txclk-tapnum`: Specify the number of delay for tx sampling.; `eswin,hsp-sp-csr`: HSP CSR is to control and get status of different high-speed peripherals (such as Ethernet, USB, SATA, etc.) via register, which can tune board-level's parameters of PHY, etc.; `eswin,drive-impedance-ohms`: Specifies the drive impedance in Ohm.; plus 1 more schema properties. Direct or nested schema references include `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `mmc-controller.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Ulf Hansson <ulf.hansson@linaro.org>, Jisheng Zhang <Jisheng.Zhang@synaptics.com>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At boot, the flattened DT node is matched through `compatible` or inherited child-node context, the relevant driver parses resources such as MMIO ranges, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, PHYs, muxes, ECC engines, partitions, or child buses, and subsystem helpers enforce the generic parts of the contract. Schema composition/control branches in this file: if=5, then=5, else=3, allOf=1, oneOf=1. Nested schema surface: no nested child-node schemas detected at the top level.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted DTS/DTB nodes encode hardware identity, resource topology, child-node layout, and phandle cell widths for long-lived boards. Tightening `required`, changing compatible ordering, changing cell counts, or altering `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties` can break existing DTBs even though no state is stored by the YAML itself.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrates with MMC core, SDHCI/DW-MSHC host-controller drivers, regulator, clock, reset, power-domain, and card-detect/write-protect bindings. Devicetree/dt-schema dependencies are the core meta-schema plus `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `mmc-controller.yaml#`. Driver integration is through OF match tables and subsystem parsers; board integration is through phandles, address/size cells, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, child flash/partition nodes, MDIO/PHY links, mux specifiers, and examples that document expected DTS shape.

**Risks:** Main risks are ABI drift, under-specified compatibles, required resources that do not match real SoC integration, wrong `#*-cells` counts, stale examples, and conditional branches that validate one variant while rejecting another valid one. For storage bindings, partition/ECC/timing mistakes can silently corrupt flash or make media unprobeable. For network bindings, PHY mode, MDIO, reset, MAC-address, or clock mistakes can leave links down. For mux and common schemas, over-permissive closure can hide misspelled properties while over-tight closure can reject legitimate inherited properties.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mmc/snps,dwcmshc-sdhci.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against in-tree DTS users for every compatible or child-node form. Verify example compilation, required-property failures, conditional branches, referenced schemas, and driver probe logs. This file contains 2 inline example block(s), which are part of dt-schema validation.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mmc/snps,dwcmshc-sdhci.yaml -->

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `SOCIONEXT Milbeaut SDHCI controller`. In this tree it is a MMC/SD/SDHCI storage host controller binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: The SOCIONEXT Milbeaut SDHCI controller is a specialized SD Host Controller found in some of Socionext's Milbeaut image processing SoCs. It features a dedicated "bridge controller." This bridge controller implements special functions like reset control, clock management for various SDR modes (SDR12, SDR25, SDR50) and physical pin property settings.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mmc/synopsys-dw-mshc-common.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Synopsys Designware Mobile Storage Host Controller Common Properties`. In this tree it is a MMC/SD/SDHCI storage host controller binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mmc/synopsys-dw-mshc-common.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include none directly enumerated; this file is primarily common schema or child-node infrastructure. Top-level required properties are none at the top level. Important properties include `resets`, `reset-names`, `clock-frequency`, `fifo-depth`, `card-detect-delay`, `data-addr`, `fifo-watermark-aligned`, `dmas`, `dma-names`. Property roles include `resets`: reset controller phandles; `reset-names`: reset line names; `dmas`: DMA channel phandles; `dma-names`: DMA channel names; `clock-frequency`: Should be the frequency (in Hz) of the ciu clock. If this is specified and the ciu clock is specified then we'll try to set the ciu clock to this at probe time.; `fifo-depth`: The maximum size of the tx/rx fifo's. If this property is not specified, the default value of the fifo size is determined from the controller registers.; `card-detect-delay`: Delay in milli-seconds before detecting card after card insert event. The default value is 0.; `data-addr`: Override fifo address with value provided by DT. The default FIFO reg offset is assumed as 0x100 (version < 0x240A) and 0x200(version >= 0x240A) by driver. If the controller does not follow this rule, please use this property to set fifo address in device tree.; `fifo-watermark-aligned`: Data done irq is expected if data length is less than watermark in PIO mode. But fifo watermark is requested to be aligned with data length in some SoC so that TX/RX irq can be generated with data done irq. Add this watermark quirk to mark this requirement and force fifo watermark setting accordingly.. Direct or nested schema references include `mmc-controller.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`. The top-level closure policy is `additionalProperties: true`. Maintainers: Ulf Hansson <ulf.hansson@linaro.org>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. A2 treated their "clock stop" bit (register offset 0x08 a.k.a. SDMMC_BUSMODE, bit 0x10) as "set 1 to disable SD clock", while all the later versions treated it as "set 0 to disable SD clock". Set this property for later versions of wm,wm8505-sdhc. On wm,wm8650-sdhc and later this property is implied and does not need to be set explicitly. Direct or nested schema references include `mmc-controller.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Alexey Charkov <alchark@gmail.com>.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/brcm,brcmnand.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Broadcom STB NAND Controller`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: The Broadcom Set-Top Box NAND controller supports low-level access to raw NAND flash chips. It has a memory-mapped register interface for both control registers and for its data input/output buffer. On some SoCs, this controller is paired with a custom DMA engine (inventively named "Flash DMA") which supports basic PROGRAM and READ functions, among other features. This controller was originally designed for STB SoCs

Use this property to describe the rare earlier versions of this core that include WP; `brcm,wp-not-connected`: Use this property when WP pin is not physically wired to the NAND chip. Write protection feature cannot be used. By default, controller assumes the pin is connected and feature is used.. The required ECC strength is automatically discoverable for some flash (e.g., according to the ONFI standard). Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `clocks`, `dmas`, `dma-names`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `clocks`: clock provider phandles; `dmas`: DMA channel phandles; `dma-names`: DMA channel names; `reg-names`: names for register regions consumed by the driver. Direct or nested schema references include `nand-controller.yaml`, `raw-nand-chip.yaml`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Ramuthevar Vadivel Murugan <vadivel.muruganx.ramuthevar@linux.intel.com>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At boot, the flattened DT node is matched through `compatible` or inherited child-node context, the relevant driver parses resources such as MMIO ranges, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, PHYs, muxes, ECC engines, partitions, or child buses, and subsystem helpers enforce the generic parts of the contract. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/jedec,spi-nor.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `SPI NOR flash ST M25Pxx (and similar) serial flash chips`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/jedec,spi-nor.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `jedec,spi-nor`, `issi,is25lp016d`, `micron,mt25qu02g`, `mxicy,mx25r1635f`, `mxicy,mx25u6435f`, `mxicy,mx25v8035f`, `spansion,s25sl12801`, `spansion,s25fs512s`. Top-level required properties are none at the top level. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `m25p,fast-read`, `broken-flash-reset`, `no-wp`, `reset-gpios`, `vcc-supply`, `spi-cpol`, `spi-cpha`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `interrupts`: interrupt line routing; `m25p,fast-read`: Use the "fast read" opcode to read data from the chip instead of the usual "read" opcode. This opcode is not supported by all chips and support for it can not be detected at runtime. Refer to your chips' datasheet to check if this is supported by your chip.; `broken-flash-reset`: Some flash devices utilize stateful addressing modes (e.g., for 32-bit addressing) which need to be managed carefully by a system. Because these sorts of flash don't have a standardized software reset command, and because some systems don't toggle the flash RESET# pin upon system reset (if the pin even exists at all), there are systems which cannot reboot properly if the flash is left in the "wrong" state. This boole; `no-wp`: The status register write disable (SRWD) bit in status register, combined with the WP# signal, provides hardware data protection for the device. When the SRWD bit is set to 1, and the WP# signal is either driven LOW or hard strapped to LOW, the status register nonvolatile bits become read-only and the WRITE STATUS REGISTER operation will not execute. The only way to exit this hardware-protected mode is to drive WP# H; `reset-gpios`: A GPIO line connected to the RESET (active low) signal of the device. If "broken-flash-reset" is present then having this property does not make any difference.; `vcc-supply`: Supply for the SPI NOR power.. Direct or nested schema references include `mtd.yaml#`, `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/mtd-physmap.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `CFI or JEDEC memory-mapped NOR flash, MTD-RAM (NVRAM...)`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: Flash chips (Memory Technology Devices) are often used for solid state file systems on embedded devices.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/mtd-physmap.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `amd,s29gl01gp`, `amd,s29gl032a`, `amd,s29gl256n`, `amd,s29gl512n`, `arm,versatile-flash`, `arm,vexpress-flash`, `cortina,gemini-flash`, `cypress,hyperflash`, `ge,imp3a-firmware-mirror`, `ge,imp3a-paged-flash`, `gef,ppc9a-firmware-mirror`, `gef,ppc9a-paged-flash`, `gef,sbc310-firmware-mirror`, `gef,sbc310-paged-flash`, `gef,sbc610-firmware-mirror`, `gef,sbc610-paged-flash`, `intel,28f128j3`, `intel,dt28f160`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `power-domains`, `bank-width`, `device-width`, `no-unaligned-direct-access`, `linux,mtd-name`, `use-advanced-sector-protection`, `erase-size`, `addr-gpios`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`, `big-endian`, `little-endian`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `clocks`: clock provider phandles; `power-domains`: generic power-domain attachment; `#address-cells`: child address cell width; `#size-cells`: child size cell width; `bank-width`: Width (in bytes) of the bank. Equal to the device width times the number of interleaved chips.; `device-width`: Width of a single mtd chip. If omitted, assumed to be equal to 'bank-width'.; `no-unaligned-direct-access`: Disables the default direct mapping of the flash. On some platforms (e.g. MPC5200) a direct 1:1 mapping may cause problems with JFFS2 usage, as the local bus (LPB) doesn't support unaligned accesses as implemented in the JFFS2 code via memcpy(). By defining "no-unaligned-direct-access", the flash will not be exposed directly to the MTD users (e.g. JFFS2) any more.; `linux,mtd-name`: Allows specifying the mtd name for retro capability with physmap-flash drivers as boot loader pass the mtd partition via the old device name physmap-flash.; `use-advanced-sector-protection`: Enables support for the advanced sector protection (Spansion: PPB - Persistent Protection Bits) locking.. Direct or nested schema references include `mtd.yaml#`, `/schemas/memory-controllers/mc-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Rob Herring <robh@kernel.org>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/mxic,multi-itfc-v009-nand-controller.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `mxic,multi-itfc-v009-nand-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `interrupts`: interrupt line routing; `clocks`: clock provider phandles; `clock-names`: clock input names; `#address-cells`: child address cell width; `#size-cells`: child size cell width. Direct or nested schema references include `nand-controller.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Mason Yang <masonccyang@mxic.com.tw>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At boot, the flattened DT node is matched through `compatible` or inherited child-node context, the relevant driver parses resources such as MMIO ranges, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, PHYs, muxes, ECC engines, partitions, or child buses, and subsystem helpers enforce the generic parts of the contract. Schema composition/control branches in this file: allOf=1. Nested schema surface: no nested child-node schemas detected at the top level.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted DTS/DTB nodes encode hardware identity, resource topology, child-node layout, and phandle cell widths for long-lived boards. Tightening `required`, changing compatible ordering, changing cell counts, or altering `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties` can break existing DTBs even though no state is stored by the YAML itself.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrates with MTD core, raw NAND/SPI-NOR/SPI-NAND/OneNAND/physmap drivers, ECC-engine providers, partition parsers, and nvmem/UBI consumers. Devicetree/dt-schema dependencies are the core meta-schema plus `nand-controller.yaml#`. Driver integration is through OF match tables and subsystem parsers; board integration is through phandles, address/size cells, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, child flash/partition nodes, MDIO/PHY links, mux specifiers, and examples that document expected DTS shape.

**Risks:** Main risks are ABI drift, under-specified compatibles, required resources that do not match real SoC integration, wrong `#*-cells` counts, stale examples, and conditional branches that validate one variant while rejecting another valid one. For storage bindings, partition/ECC/timing mistakes can silently corrupt flash or make media unprobeable. For network bindings, PHY mode, MDIO, reset, MAC-address, or clock mistakes can leave links down. For mux and common schemas, over-permissive closure can hide misspelled properties while over-tight closure can reject legitimate inherited properties.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/mxic,multi-itfc-v009-nand-controller.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against in-tree DTS users for every compatible or child-node form. Verify example compilation, required-property failures, conditional branches, referenced schemas, and driver probe logs. This file contains 1 inline example block(s), which are part of dt-schema validation.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/nand-controller-legacy.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `NAND Controller Common Properties`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: The NAND controller should be represented with its own DT node, and all NAND chips attached to this controller should be defined as children nodes of the NAND controller. This representation should be enforced even for simple controllers supporting only one chip. This is only for legacy nand controller, new controller should use nand-controller.yaml

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/nand-controller-legacy.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include none directly enumerated; this file is primarily common schema or child-node infrastructure. Top-level required properties are none at the top level. Important properties include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`, `cs-gpios`, `partitions`. Property roles include `#address-cells`: child address cell width; `#size-cells`: child size cell width; `ranges`: schema-constrained Devicetree property; `cs-gpios`: Array of chip-select available to the controller. The first entries are a 1:1 mapping of the available chip-select on the NAND controller (even if they are not used). As many additional chip-select as needed may follow and should be phandles of GPIO lines. 'reg' entries of the NAND chip subnodes become indexes of this array when this property is present.; `partitions`: flash partition child container. Direct or nested schema references include `raw-nand-chip.yaml#`, `/schemas/mtd/partitions/partition.yaml#/$defs/partition-node`, `raw-nand-property.yaml#`, `nand-property.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `additionalProperties: true`. Maintainers: Miquel Raynal <miquel.raynal@bootlin.com>, Richard Weinberger <richard@nod.at>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. Tightening `required`, changing compatible ordering, changing cell counts, or altering `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties` can break existing DTBs even though no state is stored by the YAML itself.

For storage bindings, partition/ECC/timing mistakes can silently corrupt flash or make media unprobeable. For mux and common schemas, over-permissive closure can hide misspelled properties while over-tight closure can reject legitimate inherited properties.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/partitions/partition.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Partition`. In this tree it is a MTD flash partition parser or partition subnode binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: This binding describes a single flash partition. Each partition must have its relative offset and size specified. Depending on partition function extra properties can be used. A partition may be dynamically allocated by a specific parser at runtime. In this specific case, a specific suffix is required to the node name. Everything after 'partition-' will be used as the partition name to compare with the one dynamicall

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/partitions/partition.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include none directly enumerated; this file is primarily common schema or child-node infrastructure. Top-level required properties are none at the top level. Important properties include `reg`, `label`, `read-only`, `lock`, `slc-mode`, `linux,rootfs`, `part-concat-next`, `align`, `align-size`, `align-end`, `compression`, `sercomm,scpart-id`, `nvmem-layout`. Property roles include `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `label`: The label / name for this partition. If omitted, the label is taken from the node name (excluding the unit address).; `read-only`: This parameter, if present, is a hint that this partition should only be mounted read-only. This is usually used for flash partitions containing early-boot firmware images or data which should not be clobbered.; `lock`: Do not unlock the partition at initialization time (not supported on all devices); `slc-mode`: This parameter, if present, allows one to emulate SLC mode on a partition attached to an MLC NAND thus making this partition immune to paired-pages corruptions; `linux,rootfs`: Marks partition that contains root filesystem to mount and boot user space from. Direct or nested schema references include `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/nvmem/layouts/nvmem-layout.yaml`, `#`. The source description says: The FLASH Image System (FIS) directory is a flash description format closely associated with the RedBoot boot loader. It uses one single flash eraseblock in the flash to store an index of all images in the flash. This block size will vary depending on flash but is typically 32 KB in size.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/raw-nand-property.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Raw NAND Chip Common Properties`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: The ECC strength and ECC step size properties define the user desires in terms of correction capability of a controller. Together, they request the ECC engine to correct {strength} bit errors per {size} bytes for a particular raw NAND chip. The interpretation of these parameters is implementation-defined, so not all implementations must support all possible combinations. However, implementations are encouraged to fur

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/raw-nand-property.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include none directly enumerated; this file is primarily common schema or child-node infrastructure. Top-level required properties are none at the top level. Important properties include `nand-ecc-placement`, `nand-ecc-mode`, `nand-bus-width`, `nand-on-flash-bbt`, `nand-ecc-maximize`, `nand-is-boot-medium`, `nand-rb`, `rb-gpios`, `wp-gpios`. Property roles include `nand-ecc-placement`: Location of the ECC bytes. This location is unknown by default but can be explicitly set to "oob", if all ECC bytes are known to be stored in the OOB area, or "interleaved" if ECC bytes will be interleaved with regular data in the main area.; `nand-ecc-mode`: NAND ECC placement/selection policy; `nand-bus-width`: Bus width to the NAND chip; `nand-on-flash-bbt`: With this property, the OS will search the device for a Bad Block Table (BBT). If not found, it will create one, reserve a few blocks at the end of the device to store it and update it as the device ages. Otherwise, the out-of-band area of a few pages of all the blocks will be scanned at boot time to find Bad Block Markers (BBM). These markers will help to build a volatile BBT in RAM.; `nand-ecc-maximize`: Whether or not the ECC strength should be maximized. The maximum ECC strength is both controller and chip dependent. The ECC engine has to select the ECC config providing the best strength and taking the OOB area size constraint into account. This is particularly useful when only the in-band area is used by the upper layers, and you want to make your NAND as reliable as possible.. Direct or nested schema references include `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mtd/ti,davinci-nand.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `TI DaVinci NAND controller`. In this tree it is a MTD flash memory controller, flash chip, ECC engine, or common flash schema binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/mtd/ti,davinci-nand.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `ti,davinci-nand`, `ti,keystone-nand`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `ti,davinci-chipselect`. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `partitions`, `ti,davinci-chipselect`, `ti,davinci-mask-ale`, `ti,davinci-mask-cle`, `ti,davinci-mask-chipsel`, `ti,davinci-ecc-bits`, `ti,davinci-ecc-mode`, `ti,davinci-nand-buswidth`, `ti,davinci-nand-use-bbt`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `partitions`: flash partition child container; `ti,davinci-chipselect`: Number of chipselect. Indicate on the davinci_nand driver which chipselect is used for accessing the nand.; `ti,davinci-mask-ale`: Mask for ALE. Needed for executing address phase. These offset will be added to the base address for the chip select space the NAND Flash device is connected to.; `ti,davinci-mask-cle`: Mask for CLE. Needed for executing command phase. These offset will be added to the base address for the chip select space the NAND Flash device is connected to.; `ti,davinci-mask-chipsel`: Mask for chipselect address. Needed to mask addresses for given chipselect.. Direct or nested schema references include `nand-controller.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`. The top-level closure policy is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: Marcus Folkesson <marcus.folkesson@gmail.com>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At boot, the flattened DT node is matched through `compatible` or inherited child-node context, the relevant driver parses resources such as MMIO ranges, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, PHYs, muxes, ECC engines, partitions, or child buses, and subsystem helpers enforce the generic parts of the contract. Schema composition/control branches in this file: allOf=1. Nested schema surface: no nested child-node schemas detected at the top level.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted DTS/DTB nodes encode hardware identity, resource topology, child-node layout, and phandle cell widths for long-lived boards. Tightening `required`, changing compatible ordering, changing cell counts, or altering `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties` can break existing DTBs even though no state is stored by the YAML itself.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrates with MTD core, raw NAND/SPI-NOR/SPI-NAND/OneNAND/physmap drivers, ECC-engine providers, partition parsers, and nvmem/UBI consumers. Devicetree/dt-schema dependencies are the core meta-schema plus `nand-controller.yaml`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`. Driver integration is through OF match tables and subsystem parsers; board integration is through phandles, address/size cells, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, child flash/partition nodes, MDIO/PHY links, mux specifiers, and examples that document expected DTS shape.

**Risks:** Main risks are ABI drift, under-specified compatibles, required resources that do not match real SoC integration, wrong `#*-cells` counts, stale examples, and conditional branches that validate one variant while rejecting another valid one. For storage bindings, partition/ECC/timing mistakes can silently corrupt flash or make media unprobeable. For mux and common schemas, over-permissive closure can hide misspelled properties while over-tight closure can reject legitimate inherited properties.

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `GPIO-based multiplexer controller`. In this tree it is a generic mux controller or mux consumer binding binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: Define what GPIO pins are used to control a multiplexer. Or several multiplexers, if the same pins control more than one multiplexer. The multiplexer state is defined as the number represented by the multiplexer GPIO pins, where the first pin is the least significant bit. An active pin is a binary 1, an inactive pin is a binary 0.

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `ASIX AX88172/AX88772 USB Ethernet Controllers`. In this tree it is a network controller, MDIO bus, Ethernet PHY, DSA-adjacent, or MAC/PHY integration binding binding: it constrains how DTS authors describe hardware, and it gives Linux OF/platform code a stable ABI for matching drivers, acquiring resources, and validating board descriptions with dt-schema. The source description says: Device tree properties for hard wired USB Ethernet devices.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/net/asix,ax88178.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Compatible strings or match identifiers include `usbb95,1720`, `usbb95,172a`, `usbb95,1780`, `usbb95,7720`, `usbb95,772a`, `usbb95,772b`, `usbb95,7e2b`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`. Important properties include `compatible`, `reg`, `local-mac-address`, `mac-address`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `local-mac-address`: programmed MAC address bytes; `mac-address`: MAC address bytes. Direct or nested schema references include `ethernet-controller.yaml#`. The top-level closure policy is `additionalProperties: false`. Maintainers: Oleksij Rempel <o.rempel@pengutronix.de>.

**Control flow:** Runtime control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and Linux subsystem drivers. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references, evaluates `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, and composition rules, then validates the examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At boot, the flattened DT node is matched through `compatible` or inherited child-node context, the relevant driver parses resources such as MMIO ranges, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, PHYs, muxes, ECC engines, partitions, or child buses, and subsystem helpers enforce the generic parts of the contract. Schema composition/control branches in this file: allOf=1. Nested schema surface: no nested child-node schemas detected at the top level.

**State and persistence behavior:** The file owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted DTS/DTB nodes encode hardware identity, resource topology, child-node layout, and phandle cell widths for long-lived boards. Tightening `required`, changing compatible ordering, changing cell counts, or altering `additionalProperties`/`unevaluatedProperties` can break existing DTBs even though no state is stored by the YAML itself.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrates with network core, phylink/PHY/MDIO subsystems, stmmac or vendor Ethernet drivers, reset/clock/regulator/PHY providers. Devicetree/dt-schema dependencies are the core meta-schema plus `ethernet-controller.yaml#`. Driver integration is through OF match tables and subsystem parsers; board integration is through phandles, address/size cells, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, child flash/partition nodes, MDIO/PHY links, mux specifiers, and examples that document expected DTS shape.

**Risks:** Main risks are ABI drift, under-specified compatibles, required resources that do not match real SoC integration, wrong `#*-cells` counts, stale examples, and conditional branches that validate one variant while rejecting another valid one. For storage bindings, partition/ECC/timing mistakes can silently corrupt flash or make media unprobeable. For network bindings, PHY mode, MDIO, reset, MAC-address, or clock mistakes can leave links down. For mux and common schemas, over-permissive closure can hide misspelled properties while over-tight closure can reject legitimate inherited properties.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/asix,ax88178.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against in-tree DTS users for every compatible or child-node form. Verify example compilation, required-property failures, conditional branches, referenced schemas, and driver probe logs. This file contains 2 inline example block(s), which are part of dt-schema validation.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/asix,ax88178.yaml -->

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Broadcom BCM4377 family PCIe Bluetooth Chips`. The source description says: This binding describes Broadcom BCM4377 family PCIe-attached bluetooth chips usually found in Apple machines. The Wi-Fi part of the chip is described in bindings/net/wireless/brcm,bcm4329-fmac.yaml.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This is declarative schema, not executable C code. The public interface is the binding ABI exposed through `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/net/bluetooth/brcm,bcm4377-bluetooth.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `description`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples`. Property roles include `compatible`: driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier; `reg`: MMIO, bus address, flash-chip select, or child register window; `brcm,board-type`: Board type of the Bluetooth chip. This is used to decouple the overall system board from the Bluetooth module and used to construct firmware and calibration data filenames. On Apple platforms, this should be the Apple module-instance codename prefixed by "apple,", e.g. "apple,atlantisb".; `brcm,taurus-cal-blob`: A per-device calibration blob for the Bluetooth radio. This should be filled in by the bootloader from platform configuration data, if necessary, and will be uploaded to the device. This blob is used if the chip stepping of the Bluetooth module supports beamforming.; `local-bd-address`: schema-constrained Devicetree property.
